We can use coordinate gradient with weights from b/M. Starting from power, p large. Due to scale invariance, we can fix log sum? We can optimize log a with objective J=∑ exp(λ b_k) /? to penalize maxima. Since n maybe 5000, we can compute b via FFT; gradient via FFT Hankel with w. Use multiplicative update with projection.

Pseudo:
```
def fast_score(a):
    if a.size ==0: return inf
    a = np.clip(a,0,None)
    s = a.sum()
    if s<=0: return inf
    a = a/s  # normalize sum 1
    n=a.size
    L=1
    while L<2*n-1: L<<=1
    fa= np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:2*n-1]
    M=b.max()
    # Due to numerical, maybe direct for small n? 
    return 2*n*M
```
Note: Evaluating with normalized sum=1 gives same score as raw (score scale invariant). We can normalize inside fast_score for optimization. But if we normalize each time, gradient for raw changes. Manage.

This is like multiplicative gradient with sum preserved to first order because mean subtracted and log update. Accept line search.

Let's derive parametric generator:
```
def make_shape(n, beta=0.5, e1=0, e2=0, e3=0, c0=0.5, start_shift=0.5):
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    x = (i + start_shift)/n  # in (0, (n-?)/n)
    base = x**(-beta)  # if beta not 0.5
    P = 1.0 + e1*x*(1.0-x) + e2*(x-0.5)**2 + e3*x**2? etc.
    a = base*P
    a = np.clip(a,0,None)
    s=a.sum(); if s<=0: zeros
    return a/s
```
Note if beta not 0.5, asymptotic score formula different. We can tune beta around 0.5.

Full local optimizer design:
Input: best a normalized length n. Maybe length not power-of-two. Use internal score (normalizes). We can refine with multiplicative gradient on smooth penalty.
Algorithm:
```
def local_improve(a, budget_s, seed):
    n=a.size
    best=a.copy(); best_score=fast_score(best)
    a=best.copy(); loga=np.log(a+eps)
    step=1e-2? actually scale? 
    for lam in [10,30,80,200]:
       for it in range(iters):
          b=M? compute b,M from a (sum normalized)
          w = exp(lam*(b-M)); w/=w.sum()
          g = hankel_weight_overlap(w,a)  # length n
          # update loga
          for trial in [step, step/2,...]:
             cand = np.exp(loga - trial*(g - g.mean())/M? )
             normalize
             score=fast_score(cand)
             if score<best_score:
                 accept, loga=log(cand), best=cand
             else if no accept: step*=0.7; break
    # random perturbations
```

```
def fast_coeffs(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float); a=np.clip(a,0,1000.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return inf, zeros
    a=a/s
    n=a.size
    L=1; target=2*n-1; while L<target: L*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,L)
    b=b[:target]
    # numerical small imag? irfft returns real; clip negative? b may have tiny negative due to FFT; set max(0)? The objective uses actual raw sequence; but for optimization, clipping negative coefficients to 0 may alter gradient. Use b=np.maximum(b,0) because theoretically nonnegative. Small negative no issue.
    M=float(b.max())
    score=2*n*M
    return score,b
```
Note: If we normalize inside, score of normalized sequence equals original. For final, we can return normalized (sum1). Evaluator sum 1 >0.01.

Function:
```
def make_param_shape(n, params):
    beta=params[0]; c=params[1]; e1=params[2]; e2=params[3]; e3=params[4]
    i=np.arange(n)
    x=(i+0.5)/n
    u=(i+c)/n
    base=np.power(u, -beta)
    P=1.0 + e1*x*(1-x) + e2*(x*(1-x))**2 + e3*(x-0.5)
    a=base*np.clip(P,0.0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return zeros
    return a/s
```
Need ensure c>0. If c lower bound 0.05. Note if c not proportional to n? c in units of index? I set c as fraction? `u=(i+c)/n`, c dimensionless fraction of n. If c=0.5, i=0 u=0.5/n. Good. If c=5, u=5/n less singular. If c small 0.01, more. Good.

Let's design local_improve function with multiple methods:
```
def local_improve(a, budget_s, rng, verbose=True):
    a = normalize(a)
    best=a.copy(); score=fast_score(a)
    n=a.size
    # Method 1: gradient with several lambda
    step=0.05
    for lam in [5,15,40,100,250]:
        loga=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
        step=0.05
        for it in range(200):
            if time>... break
            sc,b=fast_coeffs(a)
            M=b.max()
            w=np.exp(lam*np.clip(b-M,0,None))
            wsum=w.sum(); if tiny continue
            w=w/wsum
            g=hankel_overlap(w,a)
            d=(g - g.mean())/M
            # Try steps [step, step*0.5]
            improved=False
            for attempt in range(5):
               trial = a*np.exp(-step*d)
               trial = normalize(trial)
               s2=fast_score(trial)
               if s2 < score: accept...
               else step*=0.7
               if step <=1e-4: break
        # after lam, maybe unbalance
    # Method 2: unbalance max lag
    for it in range(5000):
       sc,b=fast_coeffs(best)
       K=np.argmax(b)
       f=0.1 maybe
       trial=unbalance_lag(best,K,f)
       s2=fast_score(trial)
       if s2<sc: best=trial; score=s2; f=min(0.9, f*1.2)
       else: f*=0.7
       if f<0.01: f=0.01
    return best,score
```

def _unbalance_lag(a,K,f):
    ca=a.copy(); n=ca.size
    if K<0 or K>=2*n-1: return ca
    half=K//2
    # diagonal if K even: maybe move mass from half to an asymmetric neighbor? For simplicity, for even K, choose pairs (half-m, half+m) for m=1.. min(half,n-1-half). This reduces contributions except diagonal. But if max even, pair diagonal cannot. We can skip even or do a small move from diagonal to nearest extreme. Let's do pairs for i=0..half where i<j valid. For even, i from 0 to half-1, j=K-i; plus diagonal term unchanged. If f moves, diagonal product a_half² remains; but pairs decrease.
    for i in range(0, half+1):
       j=K-i
       if i<j and 0<=j<n:
          x=ca[i]; y=ca[j]; s=x+y
          if s<=0: continue
          if x>=y:
             ny=y*(1-f); ny=max(0,ny); ca[i]=s-ny; ca[j]=ny
          else: ...
    return _normalize(ca)
```

Implement gradient with exact line search:
```
def _gradient_step(a, b, M, lam):
   w=np.exp(lam*(b-M)); wsum=w.sum(); if wsum<=0: return a, None
   w=w/wsum
   g=_hankel_overlap(w,a)
   dm=(g - g.mean())/M
   return dm
```

Nelder-Mead implementation details:
```
def _nelder_mead(n, p0, p_low, p_high, max_iter, deadline, verbose=False):
    D=len(p0)
    p0=np.clip(np.array(p0,float),p_low,p_high)
    f0=_param_score(p0,n)
    points=[p0]
    scores=[f0]
    # initial simplex
    base_steps = np.zeros(D)
    for d in range(D):
      width = p_high[d]-p_low[d]
      base_steps[d] = width*0.1
    for d in range(D):
      p = p0.copy(); p[d] += base_steps[d]; p=np.clip(p,lo,hi)
      f=_param_score(p,n); append
    best_point, best_score = min...
    stagi=0; max_stag=20?
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time>deadline: break
       sort points/scores
       if scores[0] < best_score: update best
       # check convergence
       if np.max(scores)-scores[0] < 1e-10: break
       # centroid of all but worst
       centroid = np.mean(points[:-1], axis=0)
       worst = points[-1]
       xr = centroid + (centroid - worst)
       xr=clip
       fr=score
       if fr < scores[0]:
          if fr < 0.75*scores[0]? expand? Standard if fr<best: expand xe=centroid+2(centroid-worst)
          else accept reflection
       elif fr < scores[-2]: accept reflection
       elif fr >= scores[-2]:
          # contract
          if fr < scores[-1]: xc = centroid + 0.5*(worst-centroid)
          else: xc = centroid -0.5*(worst-centroid)
          fx=score
          if fx < min(fr,scores[-1]): accept else shrink all points toward best
       # remove worst and add new
    return best_point,best_score
```
We need handle invalid infinite. Compare with epsilon. To avoid infinite loops, if bad many shrink.

```
def _hill_climb_params(n,p,bounds,time):
   p=clip; f=score
   step_sizes = array(widths*0.2)
   no_improve=0
   for iter in range(max_iter):
      # random coordinate or full
      idx=rng.integers(D)
      base=p.copy(); base[idx]+=rng.uniform(-1,1)*step_sizes[idx]
      f2=score; if f2<f: p=base; f=f2; no=0; maybe increase step for coord; else no++
      if no>D*5: reduce step; if step small break
      # also global resets to previous bests
```

Implementation:
```
def _hill_climb_param(n, p0, lo, hi, max_iter, deadline, rng):
    D=len(p0); p=clip; f=_param_score(p,n); bestp=p.copy(); bestf=f
    width=np.maximum(hi-lo,1e-6); steps=width*0.2
    no=0
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
       # choose perturbation
       if rng.random()<0.2:
          newp = p + rng.normal(0,1,D)*steps*0.5
       elif rng.random()<0.2:
          newp = p.copy()
          idx=int(rng.integers(D)); newp[idx]=rng.uniform(lo[idx], hi[idx])
       else:
          newp=p.copy(); idx=int(rng.integers(D)); newp[idx]=p[idx]+rng.normal(0,1)*steps[idx]
       newp=np.clip(newp, lo,hi)
       f2=_param_score(newp,n)
       if f2 < f - 1e-12:
          p=newp; f=f2; no=0
          if rng.random()<0.5: steps[idx]=min(width[idx]*0.6, steps[idx]*1.3)
          if f2 < bestf: bestf=f2; bestp=newp.copy()
       else:
          no +=1
          if no % 7 ==0:
             # damp
             idx_imp = D-1? Use coordinate? We don't know; damp all
             steps = np.maximum(steps*0.8, width*1e-4)
          # occasionally try step increase? no
       if not np.isfinite(f2):
          steps=np.maximum(steps*0.5,width*1e-4)
    # final coordinate refine: for each dimension, try small grid around bestp
    for dim in range(D):
       if time...:
          base=bestp.copy()
          center=base[dim]
          # sample 9 points between center±5*steps[dim]
          for val in linspace(center-steps[dim]*3, center+steps[dim]*3, 9):
              cand=base.copy(); cand[dim]=val; f2=score; update best
    return bestp,bestf
```
This may not be optimal but okay.

Maybe use scipy if available to improve. We can try after custom? If time left >100s, call scipy minimize from bestp with maxiter based on time? But uncertain. Let's not to keep deterministic.

Local unbalance implementation details to control time:
```
def _unbalance_local(a, deadline, rng):
    best=a.copy(); score,b=_fft_score(best); f=0.1; n=best.size
    no=0
    while time < deadline:
       # choose K
       M=b.max()
       # top indices
       if len(b)>20:
          top_idx=np.argpartition(b, -20)[-20:]
       else: top_idx=np.arange(len(b))
       K=int(top_idx[int(rng.integers(len(top_idx)))])
       # optional if K near edge and no improve, choose central? 
       trial=_unbalance_lag(best,K,f)
       s2,b2=_fft_score(trial)
       if s2 + 1e-14 < score:
          best=trial; score=s2; b=b2; f=min(0.9, f*1.05); no=0
       else:
          f=max(0.02, f*0.8); no+=1
          if no>50:
             f=0.1; no=0
             # try reverse? Could balance for a top K? Maybe not
       # To avoid recomputing b if reject: b stays. But if K repeated and reject, try different top? The loop chooses new random each time from same b.
    return best,score
```

```
def _fft_score(a, normalize=True):
    arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if normalize:
       s=arr.sum(); if s<=0: return inf,None; arr=arr/s
    else: s=arr.sum(); if s<=0: return inf,None; arr=arr/s  # score invariant, normalize for consistency
```
Cannot skip because b scale; but score invariant; for gradient use normalized a. Local updates normalize.
Could compute b using `fa=np.fft.rfft(arr, L); b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,L)[:2*n-1]`. Setting negative to 0: `b = np.maximum(b,0)`. In large FFT, roundoff may make some negative -1e-16; max not affected.

`_hankel_overlap(w,a):`
```
n=a.size
L=1; need=n+2*n-1-1=3*n-2
while L<need: L<<=1
ar=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], L)
wr=np.fft.rfft(w, L)
c=np.fft.irfft(ar*wr,L)
return c[n-1:2*n-2]
```
Check slice length: n-1 to 2n-2 inclusive length n? c indices [n-1, n, ..., 2n-2] length n. np slice end exclusive 2n-1? Actually c[n-1:2*n-1] length (2n-1)-(n-1)=n. Need 2n-1. Since max index 2n-2, slice end 2n-1. Use `c[n-1:2*n-1]`.

Unbalance: For K, valid pairs. If K<0 or K>=2n-1 return. Let start=max(0, K-(n-1)), end=min(n-1,K). We need pairs i<j within [start,end]. If K even, diagonal i=K//2 skip. Loop i from start to (K-1)//2.
```
for i in range(start, end+1):
   j=K-i
   if i>=j: continue
   x=ca[i]; y=ca[j]; s=x+y
   if s<=0: continue
   if x>=y:
       y *= (1-f); y=max(0,y); ca[j]=y; ca[i]=s-y
   else:
       x *= (1-f); ...
```

Gradient function: Let's implement conservatively optional if n<=12000.
```
def _gradient_improve(a, deadline):
    best=a.copy(); score,b=_fft_score(best); n=best.size; logbest=np.log(np.maximum(best,1e-300))
    eta=0.05
    no=0
    for lam in [5,15,40,100]:
       if time>deadline: break
       improved_any=False
       for it in range(100):
          if time>deadline: break
          M=b.max()
          if M<=0: break
          w=np.exp(lam*(b-M)); wsum=w.sum()
          if wsum<=0: break
          w=w/wsum
          g=_hankel_overlap(w,best)
          d=(g - np.mean(g))/M
          # clip d to avoid huge
          d=np.clip(d, -50, 50)
          trial_best=None; score_best=score; next_eta=eta
          for _ in range(4):
             trial=np.exp(np.clip(logbest - eta*d, -50, 50))
             trial=_normalize(trial)
             if not finite: break
             s2,b2=_fft_score(trial)
             if s2 + 1e-14 < score_best:
                trial_best=trial; score_best=s2; b2local=b2; eta=max(eta*1.1, 0.01)??; next_eta=eta
                break
             else:
                eta*=0.5
             if eta<1e-3: break
          if trial_best is None:
             # try opposite sign? maybe if eta small
             break
          else:
             best=trial_best; logbest=np.log(max(best,eps)); score=score_best; b=b2local; improved_any=True; no=0
       if not improved_any: break
    return best,score
```
Need not modify eta across lam? We can reset eta=0.05 each lam/no. The gradient direction can be wrong (increase). If score never improves, break. def _improve_local(a, deadline, rng):
   score,b=_fft_score(a); best=a.copy()
   # unbalance 50% of available
   now=time; avail=deadline-now
   if avail>2:
       sub=now+0.45*avail

   # gradient 30%

Let's set `_unbalance_local` bound it max iterations and no-improve stop. Use `max_iter` parameter maybe 30000 but time. It will stop if no improve many times.
```
def _unbalance_local(a, deadline, rng, max_no=80, max_iter=500000):
    best=a.copy(); score,b=_fft_score(best)
    n=best.size; f=0.1; no=0; iters=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and iters<max_iter:
       iters+=1
       if no > max_no: # try restart f and maybe smooth
           f=0.1; no=0
           # try a simple noise? maybe break?
           if iters>1000: break
       # choose K
       if rng.random()<0.5:
           K=n-1
       else:
           M=b.max()
           # top by argpartition
           if b.size>50:
              top=np.argpartition(b, -50)[-50:]
           else: top=np.arange(b.size)
           # choose weighted? random
           K=int(top[int(rng.integers(len(top)))])
       # choose f maybe adapt; sometimes random in [0.05,0.7]
       trial_f=f
       if rng.random()<0.1:
          trial_f=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.8))
       trial=_unbalance_lag(best,K,trial_f)
       s2,b2=_fft_score(trial)
       if s2 + 1e-15 < score:
          best=trial; score=s2; b=b2
          f=min(0.9, f*1.05)
          no=0
       else:
          # if random larger failed, reduce
          no+=1
          if no % 5 ==0:
             f=max(0.02, f*0.8)
    return best,score
```

Code details:
```
def _next_pow2(m):
    L=1
    while L<m: L <<=1
    return L
```

`_normalize`:
```
def _normalize(a):
    arr=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    arr=np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if arr.size ==0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
         return np.zeros(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    s=arr.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
         return np.zeros(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    arr=arr/s
    return arr
```
But if arr contains nan, np.clip with nan gives nan; np.all finite false. Use `if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)): arr=np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)`. But inputs should finite.

`_fft_score`:
```
def _fft_score(a):
    arr=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size==0: return np.inf, None
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    arr[arr<0]=0? clip handles
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
       arr=np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0.0:
       return np.inf, None
    arr=arr/s
    n=arr.size
    L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(arr, L)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)
    b=b[:2*n-1]
    b=np.maximum(b,0.0)
    M=float(b.max())
    score=float(2.0*n*M)
    if not np.isfinite(score): return np.inf,b
    return score,b
```
Potential overflow in fa*fa if arr large? arr normalized sum=1, max maybe large if concentrated, could be up to 1? Actually normalized max ≤1 if nonneg sum=1, so safe.

`_score`: returns `_fft_score(a)[0]`.

`_top_indices`:
```
def _top_indices(b,k=20):
    b=np.asarray(b)
    m=b.size
    if k>=m: return np.arange(m)
    return np.argpartition(b, -k)[-k:]
```
If b has NaN? no.

`_hankel_overlap`: If w size !=2n-1? We pass b length 2n-1. Make w sum=1. If sum zero, return zeros. In function:
```
ws=float(w.sum())
if ws<=0: return zeros
w=w/ws
```
Then `need=n + len(w) -1` where len(w)=2n-1 =>3n-2. L next pow2. `ar=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], L)`; `wr=np.fft.rfft(w, L)`; `c=np.fft.irfft(ar*wr,L)`. `out=c[n-1:2*n-1]`. Ensure length n: for n=1, K? central? We won't use n=1. For n=1 slice c[0:1] ok. Clip negative small: `out=np.maximum(out,0.0)`.

`_make_param_shape`:
```
def _make_param_shape(n, params):
    if n <=0: return []
    p=params
    beta=float(p[0]); c=float(p[1]); e1=float(p[2]); e2=float(p[3]); e3=float(p[4])
    c=max(1e-3,c)
    i=np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x=(i+0.5)/float(n)
    u=(i+c)/float(n)
    base=np.power(u, -beta)
    t_one_minus = x*(1.0-x)
    P=1.0 + e1*t_one_minus + e2*t_one_minus*t_one_minus + e3*(x-0.5)
    a=base*P
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
       a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
       # fallback simple power
       i=np.arange(n); a=i? 
    return _normalize(a)
```
If P negative clipped; if all zero fallback 1/√u.

`_hill_climb_param`: Need import `operator`? no. In coordinate refine, no lambda.

```
def _hill_climb_param(n,p0,lo,hi,max_iter,deadline,rng):
    D=len(p0); lo=np.asarray(lo,float); hi=np.asarray(hi,float)
    p=np.clip(np.asarray(p0,float),lo,hi)
    f=_param_score(p,n)
    bestp=p.copy(); bestf=f
    width=np.maximum(hi-lo,1e-6)
    steps=width*0.25
    no=0; restarts=0; it=0
    while it<max_iter and time.monotonic()<deadline:
       it+=1
       # if f inf, reset
       if not np.isfinite(f):
          p=bestp.copy() if np.isfinite(bestf) else np.clip((lo+hi)/2,lo,hi); f=_param_score(p,n); steps=width*0.25; no=0
       r=rng.random()
       if r < 0.15:
          newp=p + rng.normal(0.0,1.0,D)*steps*0.6
       elif r < 0.3:
          newp=p.copy(); idx=int(rng.integers(D)); newp[idx]=float(rng.uniform(lo[idx],hi[idx]))
       else:
          newp=p.copy(); idx=int(rng.integers(D)); newp[idx]=p[idx]+float(rng.normal(0.0,1.0))*steps[idx]
       newp=np.clip(newp,lo,hi)
       f2=_param_score(newp,n)
       if np.isfinite(f2) and f2 < f - 1e-12:
          p=newp; f=f2; no=0
          # make step larger for successful direction sometimes
          if len(newp)<...? We don't know coordinate if full; if coord, can increase. But optional.
          if f2 < bestf:
             bestf=f2; bestp=newp.copy()
       else:
          no+=1
          if no % 10 ==0:
             steps=np.maximum(steps*0.85, width*1e-4)
          if no > 200 and restarts <5:
             p=bestp.copy(); f=bestf; steps=width*0.2; no=0; restarts+=1
       if np.all(steps < width*1e-4) and no>100:
          break
    # coordinate refine around bestp
    for dim in range(D):
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       base=bestp.copy()
       center=base[dim]
       spread=max(steps[dim], width[dim]*0.05, (hi[dim]-lo[dim])*0.02)
       for mval in (-3,-2,-1,0,1,2,3):
          cand=base.copy(); cand[dim]=center + mval*spread
          cand=np.clip(cand,lo,hi)
          fc=_param_score(cand,n)
          if np.isfinite(fc) and fc < bestf - 1e-12:
             bestf=fc; bestp=cand.copy()
       # one local coordinate random? enough
    return bestp,bestf
```
This may be slow due coord refine 5*7=35 evals; fine.

We need in no-improve steps, if full perturbation success, cannot know idx; not important.

`_gradient_improve`: Need no lambda. Use time variable.
```
def _gradient_improve(a, deadline, rng):
    best=a.copy(); score,b=_fft_score(best)
    n=best.size
    if n>15000 or time... return best,score
    logbest=np.log(np.maximum(best,1e-300))
    for lam in (5.0,15.0,40.0,100.0):
       eta=0.08; sign=-1; no=0
       for it in range(150):
          if time>deadline: break
          M=float(b.max())
          if M<=0 or not finite: break
          z=lam*(b-M)
          # clip z maybe -80,0
          z=np.clip(z, -80.0, 0.0)
          w=np.exp(z)
          ws=w.sum()
          if ws<=0 or not finite: break
          w=w/ws
          g=_hankel_overlap(w,best)
          scaled=(g - np.mean(g))/M
          scaled=np.clip(scaled, -50.0, 50.0)
          improved=False
          for tries in range(5):
             trial=np.exp(np.clip(logbest + sign*eta*scaled, -50,50))
             trial=_normalize(trial)
             if trial.size==0: break
             s2,b2=_fft_score(trial)
             if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 + 1e-14 < score:
                best=trial; score=s2; b=b2; logbest=np.log(np.maximum(best,1e-300)); improved=True; eta=min(0.3, eta*1.2); break
             else:
                eta*=0.6
             if eta<1e-3: break
          if improved:
             no=0
          else:
             no+=1
             if no>20:
                sign *=-1
                eta=0.08; no=0
             if no>60: break
    return best,score
```
If sign flips, maybe recurrence. Could be okay. The gradient direction might be opposite for lam; line search finds if any.

Potential issue: For lam=5, weights broad; update may not improve. no>20 flips sign many. Could be slow but limited.

Maybe gradient not improve; fine.

def _unbalance_local(a, deadline, rng):
   best=a.copy(); score,b=_fft_score(best)
   if not finite b: return best,score
   n=best.size
   top=_top_indices(b, 30)
   f=0.1; no=0; it=0; max_iter=200000
   while time.monotonic()<deadline and it<max_iter:
      it+=1
      if no > 300:
         break
      if rng.random()<0.45:
         K=n-1
      else:
         if top.size==0 or no>100:
            top=_top_indices(b,30)
         K=int(top[int(rng.integers(top.size))])
      trial_f=f
      if rng.random()<0.08:
         trial_f=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.8))
      trial=_unbalance_lag(best,K,trial_f)
      s2,b2=_fft_score(trial)
      if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 + 1e-14 < score:
         best=trial; score=s2; b=b2; top=_top_indices(b,30); no=0; f=min(0.9, f*1.08)
      else:
         no +=1
         if no % 10 ==0:
            f=max(0.02, f*0.8)
         if no > 200 and f <=0.03:
            # maybe try larger jump
            f=0.5
      if no > 500: break
   return best,score
```

def _improve_local(a, deadline, rng):
   best=a.copy(); score,b=_fft_score(best)
   if not finite: return best,score
   now=time.monotonic(); avail=deadline-now
   if avail <=1: return best,score
   sub=now+0.35*avail
   a2,s2=_unbalance_local(best, min(sub,deadline), rng)
   if finite and s2<score: best=a2; score=s2
   now=time.monotonic(); avail=deadline-now
   if avail>5 and best.size<=12000:
       sub2=now+0.25*avail
       a3,s3=_gradient_improve(best, min(sub2,deadline), rng)
       if finite and s3<score: best=a3; score=s3
   now=time.monotonic()
   if now+1 < deadline:
       a4,s4=_unbalance_local(best, deadline-1.0, rng)
       if finite and s4<score: best=a4; score=s4
   return best,score
```

def _add_candidate(cands, best_seq, best_score, seq, score):
    if not np.isfinite(score): return best_seq,best_score
    if score < best_score - 1e-15:
       best_seq=seq.copy(); best_score=score
    # insert into cands if not duplicates? just cands list of (score,seq)
    if len(cands) < 8:
       cands.append((score, seq.copy()))
    else:
       worst=0; ws=cands[0][0]
       for i in range(1,len(cands)):
          if cands[i][0] > ws: worst=i; ws=cands[i][0]
       if score < ws:
          cands[worst]=(score, seq.copy())
    return best_seq,best_score

def _initial_scan(n, deadline):
    results=[]
    betas=(0.50,0.52,0.54)
    cs=(0.1,0.5,1.0)
    e1s=(-0.5,0.0,0.5)
    e3s=(-0.4,-0.2,0.0,0.2)
    for b in betas:
      for c in cs:
       for e1 in e1s:
        for e3 in e3s:
          if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
          p=[b,c,e1,0.0,e3]
          try: seq=np.asarray(_make_param_shape(n,p), dtype=float); sc,_=_fft_score(seq)
          except Exception: continue
          if np.isfinite(sc):
             item=(sc,seq.copy(),p.copy())
             if len(results)<8: results.append(item)
             else:
                worst=0; ws=results[0][0]
                for i in range(1,len(results)):
                    if results[i][0] > ws: worst=i; ws=results[i][0]
                if sc < ws: results[worst]=item
    _sort_by_score(results)
    return results
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative coefficient sequences minimizing

    2 * n * max(convolution(a, a)) / (sum(a)**2)

with a combination of:
  - analytic front-loaded fractional-power templates,
  - low-dimensional template parameter search,
  - paired mass-unbalancing local search around active convolution lags,
  - conservative multiplicative gradient steps for smooth refinement.

The iterative local search uses fast Fourier transforms for autocoefficients
and requires only numpy.  The final sequence is normalized to sum 1, so it is
scale invariant and valid for the supplied evaluator.
"""

import time
import numpy as np


def _sort_by_score(items):
    """Stable enough simple selection sort for small lists, no lambdas."""
    n = len(items)
    for i in range(n):
        minj = i
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            if items[j][0] < items[minj][0]:
                minj = j
        if minj != i:
            items[i], items[minj] = items[minj], items[i]


def _normalize(a):
    """Return a non-negative finite array with sum 1."""
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return arr.copy()
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros(arr.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return arr / s


def _next_pow2(m):
    L = 1
    while L < m:
        L <<= 1
    return L


def _fft_score(a):
    """
    Fast score evaluation using FFT.

    Returns (score, convolution_coeffs). The convolution coefficients are
    computed after normalizing sum(a) = 1, which gives the same scale-invariant
    objective as the evaluator.
    """
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.inf, None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.inf, None
    arr = arr / s
    n = arr.size
    target = 2 * n - 1
    L = _next_pow2(target)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(arr, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)
    b = b[:target]
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    M = float(b.max())
    score = float(2.0 * n * M)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return np.inf, b
    return score, b


def _score(a):
    """Return only the scalar score."""
    return _fft_score(a)[0]


def _is_better(seq, score, other_seq, other_score):
    """Prefer lower score; on near-ties prefer shorter sequences."""
    if (not np.isfinite(score)):
        return False
    if (not np.isfinite(other_score)) or score < other_score - 1e-12:
        return True
    if abs(score - other_score) <= 1e-10:
        if other_seq is not None and seq is not None:
            return int(seq.size) < int(other_seq.size)
    return False


def _add_candidate(cands, best_seq, best_score, seq, score, max_keep=10):
    """Maintain current best and a small list of candidate sequences."""
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if seq.size == 0 or (not np.isfinite(score)):
        return best_seq, best_score

    if _is_better(seq, score, best_seq, best_score):
        best_seq = seq.copy()
        best_score = float(score)

    item = (float(score), seq.copy())
    if len(cands) < max_keep:
        cands.append(item)
    else:
        worst = 0
        ws = cands[0][0]
        for i in range(1, len(cands)):
            if cands[i][0] > ws or (
                abs(cands[i][0] - ws) <= 1e-12 and
                cands[i][1].size > cands[worst][1].size
            ):
                worst = i
                ws = cands[i][0]
        if (score < ws - 1e-12) or (
            abs(score - ws) <= 1e-10 and seq.size < cands[worst][1].size
        ):
            cands[worst] = item
    return best_seq, best_score


def _top_indices(b, k=30):
    """Return indices of k largest entries of b (a cheap random view)."""
    b = np.asarray(b)
    m = b.size
    if m == 0 or k <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=int)
    if k >= m:
        return np.arange(m, dtype=int)
    return np.argpartition(b, -k)[-k:].astype(int)


def _make_fallback_sequence(n=4096):
    """Simple valid starting point: one-sided square-root taper."""
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x = (i + 0.5) / float(n)
    return _normalize(x ** -0.5)


def _make_param_shape(n, params):
    """
    Construct a positive sequence from a low-dimensional template.

    params = [beta, c, e1, e2, e3]

        a_i ~ ( (i+c)/n )^{-beta} *
              [1 + e1*x*(1-x) + e2*(x*(1-x))^2 + e3*(x-1/2)],
        x = (i+1/2)/n.

    The unscaled array is normalized to sum 1.
    """
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    p = list(params)
    while len(p) < 5:
        p.append(0.0)
    beta = float(p[0])
    c = max(1e-3, float(p[1]))
    e1 = float(p[2])
    e2 = float(p[3])
    e3 = float(p[4])

    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x = (i + 0.5) / float(n)
    u = (i + c) / float(n)

    base = np.power(u, -beta)
    xm = x * (1.0 - x)
    P = 1.0 + e1 * xm + e2 * xm * xm + e3 * (x - 0.5)
    a = base * P
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)

    s = float(a.sum())
    if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 0.0:
        # Robust fallback inside the template.
        i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
        x = (i + 0.5) / float(n)
        a = x ** -0.5
    return _normalize(a)


def _param_score(params, n):
    a = _make_param_shape(n, params)
    return _score(a)


def _initial_scan(n, deadline):
    """A small deterministic scan to seed the parameter search."""
    results = []
    betas = (0.50, 0.52, 0.54)
    cs = (0.1, 0.5, 1.0)
    e1s = (-0.5, 0.0, 0.5)
    e3s = (-0.4, -0.2, 0.0, 0.2)

    stop = time.monotonic() > deadline

    for beta in betas:
        if stop:
            break
        for c in cs:
            if stop:
                break
            for e1 in e1s:
                if stop:
                    break
                for e3 in e3s:
                    stop = time.monotonic() > deadline
                    if stop:
                        break
                    p = [beta, c, e1, 0.0, e3]
                    try:
                        a = _make_param_shape(n, p)
                        sc, _ = _fft_score(a)
                    except Exception:
                        continue
                    if np.isfinite(sc):
                        item = (float(sc), a.copy(), list(p))
                        if len(results) < 10:
                            results.append(item)
                        else:
                            worst = 0
                            ws = results[0][0]
                            for j in range(1, len(results)):
                                if results[j][0] > ws:
                                    worst = j
                                    ws = results[j][0]
                            if sc < ws:
                                results[worst] = item
    _sort_by_score(results)
    return results


def _hill_climb_param(n, p0, p_low, p_high, max_iter, deadline, rng):
    """
    Adaptive random/hill-climb search over the five template parameters.
    It deliberately uses multiple perturbation styles to avoid very narrow
    basins.
    """
    low = np.asarray(p_low, dtype=np.float64)
    high = np.asarray(p_high, dtype=np.float64)
    D = len(p0)

    p = np.clip(np.asarray(p0, dtype=np.float64), low, high)
    f = _param_score(p, n)
    bestp = p.copy()
    bestf = f

    width = np.maximum(high - low, 1e-6)
    steps = width * 0.25
    no = 0
    restarts = 0
    it = 0

    while it < max_iter and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1

        if (not np.isfinite(f)) or (not np.isfinite(bestf)):
            if np.isfinite(bestf):
                p = bestp.copy()
                f = bestf
            else:
                p = np.clip((low + high) * 0.5, low, high)
                f = _param_score(p, n)
                bestp = p.copy()
                bestf = f
            steps = width * 0.25
            no = 0
            continue

        r = float(rng.random())
        if r < 0.15:
            newp = p + rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, D) * steps * 0.6
        elif r < 0.30:
            newp = p.copy()
            idx = int(rng.integers(D))
            newp[idx] = float(rng.uniform(low[idx], high[idx]))
        else:
            newp = p.copy()
            idx = int(rng.integers(D))
            newp[idx] = p[idx] + float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0)) * steps[idx]

        newp = np.clip(newp, low, high)
        f2 = _param_score(newp, n)

        if np.isfinite(f2) and f2 < f - 1e-12:
            p = newp
            f = float(f2)
            no = 0
            if f < bestf - 1e-14:
                bestf = f
                bestp = p.copy()
        else:
            no += 1
            if no % 10 == 0:
                steps = np.maximum(steps * 0.85, width * 1e-4)
            if no > 220 and restarts < 5:
                if np.isfinite(bestf):
                    p = bestp.copy()
                    f = float(bestf)
                    steps = width * 0.20
                    no = 0
                    restarts += 1

        if float(np.max(width * 1e-4)) > 0.0 and no > 120 and np.all(steps < width * 1e-4):
            break

    # Coordinate refactor: small one-dimensional sweeps around the best point.
    for dim in range(D):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        base = bestp.copy()
        center = float(base[dim])
        spread = max(float(steps[dim]), float(width[dim]) * 0.04)
        for mm in (-4, -3, -2, -1, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4):
            cand = base.copy()
            cand[dim] = center + float(mm) * spread
            cand = np.clip(cand, low, high)
            fc = _param_score(cand, n)
            if np.isfinite(fc) and fc < bestf - 1e-13:
                bestf = float(fc)
                bestp = cand.copy()

    return bestp, float(bestf)


def _hankel_overlap(w, a):
    """
    Compute g_i = sum_j a_j w_{i+j}, where w has length 2n-1.

    This is the derivative of weighted sum of convolution coefficients with
    respect to a_i, up to the factor 2.
    """
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if w.size != 2 * n - 1:
        # Fallback: truncate/pad defensively.
        ww = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(w.size, ww.size)
        ww[:m] = w[:m]
        w = ww
    ws = float(w.sum())
    if (not np.isfinite(ws)) or ws <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    w = w / ws

    need = n + w.size - 1
    L = _next_pow2(need)
    ar = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], L)
    wr = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(ar * wr, L)
    out = c[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    return np.maximum(out, 0.0)


def _unbalance_lag(a, K, f):
    """
    For a fixed convolution index K, move a fraction f of the smaller element
    of each pair (i, K-i) into the larger element.  This reduces the product
    i + j = K for that pair, which directly attacks an active coefficient.
    """
    n = int(a.size)
    K = int(K)
    if K < 0 or K > 2 * n - 2 or n <= 1:
        return np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()

    ca = np.array(a, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
    start = max(0, K - (n - 1))
    end = min(n - 1, K)
    f = float(np.clip(f, 0.0, 0.999))

    for i in range(start, end + 1):
        j = K - i
        if i >= j:
            continue
        if i >= n or j >= n:
            continue
        x = ca[i]
        y = ca[j]
        s = x + y
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
            continue
        if x >= y:
            ny = y * (1.0 - f)
            ny = max(0.0, ny)
            ca[j] = ny
            ca[i] = s - ny
        else:
            nx = x * (1.0 - f)
            nx = max(0.0, nx)
            ca[i] = nx
            ca[j] = s - nx

    return _normalize(ca)


def _unbalance_local(a, deadline, rng):
    """Targeted local search via paired unbalancing of active lags."""
    best = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    score, b = _fft_score(best)
    if (not np.isfinite(score)) or b is None:
        return best, score

    n = int(best.size)
    top = _top_indices(b, 35)
    f = 0.15
    no = 0
    it = 0
    max_iter = 300000

    while time.monotonic() < deadline and it < max_iter:
        it += 1
        if no > 500:
            break

        if float(rng.random()) < 0.45:
            K = n - 1
        else:
            if top.size == 0 or no > 120:
                top = _top_indices(b, 35)
            if top.size == 0:
                top = np.array([n - 1], dtype=int)
            K = int(top[int(rng.integers(top.size))])

        trial_f = f
        if float(rng.random()) < 0.10:
            trial_f = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.85))

        try:
            trial = _unbalance_lag(best, K, trial_f)
            s2, b2 = _fft_score(trial)
        except Exception:
            s2 = np.inf
            b2 = b

        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < score - 1e-14:
            best = trial
            score = float(s2)
            b = b2
            top = _top_indices(b, 35)
            no = 0
            f = min(0.9, f * 1.08)
        else:
            no += 1
            if no % 12 == 0:
                f = max(0.02, f * 0.78)

            # Occasionally perturb a random block to escape a rut.
            if no > 180 and float(rng.random()) < 0.15:
                try:
                    cand = best.copy()
                    width = int(rng.integers(max(1, n // 180), max(2, n // 12)))
                    st = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
                    st = max(0, min(n - width, st))
                    cand[st:st + width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.07)))
                    cand = _normalize(cand)
                    s3, b3 = _fft_score(cand)
                    if np.isfinite(s3) and s3 < score - 1e-14:
                        best = cand
                        score = float(s3)
                        b = b3
                        top = _top_indices(b, 35)
                        no = 0
                        f = 0.15
                except Exception:
                    pass

    return best, float(score)


def _gradient_improve(a, deadline, rng):
    """
    Optional smooth local refinement.

    Uses a softmax-weighted sum of convolution coefficients and multiplicative
    updates in log-space.  Because the problem is non-convex, this is kept
    conservative and only accepted by exact fast-score line search.
    """
    best = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    score, b = _fft_score(best)
    if (not np.isfinite(score)) or b is None:
        return best, score

    n = int(best.size)
    if n > 15000:
        return best, score

    logbest = np.log(np.maximum(best, 1e-300))

    for lam in (5.0, 15.0, 40.0, 90.0):
        eta = 0.08
        sign = -1.0
        no = 0

        for it in range(90):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break

            M = float(b.max())
            if (not np.isfinite(M)) or M <= 0.0:
                break

            z = lam * (b - M)
            z = np.clip(z, -80.0, 0.0)
            w = np.exp(z)
            ws = float(w.sum())
            if (not np.isfinite(ws)) or ws <= 0.0:
                break
            w = w / ws

            try:
                g = _hankel_overlap(w, best)
            except Exception:
                break

            scaled = (g - float(np.mean(g))) / M
            scaled = np.clip(scaled, -60.0, 60.0)

            improved = False
            for _try in range(4):
                exponent = sign * eta * scaled
                exponent = np.clip(exponent, -50.0, 50.0)
                trial = np.exp(logbest + exponent)
                trial = _normalize(trial)
                if trial.size != n:
                    break
                s2, b2 = _fft_score(trial)
                if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < score - 1e-14:
                    best = trial
                    score = float(s2)
                    b = b2
                    logbest = np.log(np.maximum(best, 1e-300))
                    improved = True
                    eta = min(0.35, eta * 1.25)
                    break
                eta *= 0.55
                if eta < 1e-3:
                    break

            if improved:
                no = 0
            else:
                no += 1
                if no > 25:
                    sign *= -1.0
                    eta = 0.08
                    no = 0
                if no > 80:
                    break

    return best, float(score)


def _improve_local(a, deadline, rng):
    """Combine unbalancing and gradient refinement, using wall-clock budget."""
    best = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    score, _ = _fft_score(best)
    if (not np.isfinite(score)):
        return best, score

    now = time.monotonic()
    avail = max(0.0, deadline - now)
    if avail <= 3.0:
        return best, score

    # First: strong paired unbalancing.
    sub1 = now + 0.38 * avail
    a2, s2 = _unbalance_local(best, min(sub1, deadline), rng)
    if _is_better(a2, s2, best, score):
        best = a2
        score = float(s2)

    now = time.monotonic()
    avail = max(0.0, deadline - now)
    if avail > 6.0 and int(best.size) <= 13000:
        sub2 = now + 0.24 * avail
        a3, s3 = _gradient_improve(best, min(sub2, deadline), rng)
        if _is_better(a3, s3, best, score):
            best = a3
            score = float(s3)

    now = time.monotonic()
    if now + 2.0 < deadline:
        a4, s4 = _unbalance_local(best, deadline - 2.0, rng)
        if _is_better(a4, s4, best, score):
            best = a4
            score = float(s4)

    return best, float(score)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Time-bounded search entry point.

    Returns a list of non-negative floats.  It always returns a valid sequence
    before the deadline.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1800.0
    budget = max(10.0, min(budget, 1800.0))
    deadline = start + budget - 12.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Safe initial sequence.
    best_seq = _make_fallback_sequence(4096)
    best_score = _score(best_seq)
    cands = [(float(best_score), best_seq.copy())]

    try:
        print(f"initial fallback score={best_score:.8f}", flush=True)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 1. Quick analytic template scan at moderate length.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    quick_params = []
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 120.0:
        nq = 4096
        quick_end = min(deadline - 100.0, start + 90.0)
        quick_res = _initial_scan(nq, quick_end)
        for item in quick_res:
            sc, seq, par = item
            best_seq, best_score = _add_candidate(cands, best_seq, best_score, seq, sc)
            quick_params.append(list(par))

        extra = [
            [0.52, 0.20, -0.35, 0.25, -0.40],
            [0.53, 0.25, -0.70, 0.10, -0.50],
            [0.50, 0.80, 0.45, -0.45, 0.10],
            [0.49, 1.50, 0.00, 0.00, 0.00],
            [0.52, 0.75, -0.20, 0.00, -0.55],
            [0.51, 0.35, 0.30, -0.30, -0.25],
        ]
        quick_params.extend(extra)

    if not quick_params:
        quick_params = [[0.5, 0.5, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0]]

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 2. Evaluate promising templates at larger lengths.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    lengths = (8192, 16384)
    param_end = min(deadline - 300.0, start + 220.0)
    for p in quick_params:
        if time.monotonic() >= param_end:
            break
        for n in lengths:
            if time.monotonic() >= param_end:
                break
            try:
                seq = _make_param_shape(n, p)
                sc, _ = _fft_score(seq)
            except Exception:
                continue
            best_seq, best_score = _add_candidate(cands, best_seq, best_score, seq, sc)

    try:
        print(f"after template evaluation best={best_score:.8f}", flush=True)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 3. Low-dimensional template optimization.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    p_low = [0.48, 0.05, -2.0, -2.0, -1.0]
    p_high = [0.60, 5.00, 3.0, 3.0, 1.0]

    p_seed = quick_params[0] if len(quick_params) > 0 else [0.5, 0.5, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0]

    hill1_end = min(deadline - 220.0, start + 620.0)
    if time.monotonic() < hill1_end - 30.0:
        try:
            p_best, f_best = _hill_climb_param(8192, p_seed, p_low, p_high, 8000, hill1_end, rng)
            seq = _make_param_shape(8192, p_best)
            sc, _ = _fft_score(seq)
            best_seq, best_score = _add_candidate(cands, best_seq, best_score, seq, sc)
            try:
                print(f"hill 8192 score={sc:.8f} params={p_best}", flush=True)
            except Exception:
                pass
        except Exception:
            p_best, f_best = p_seed, np.inf

        hill2_end = min(deadline - 90.0, start + 880.0)
        if time.monotonic() < hill2_end - 30.0:
            try:
                p_best2, f_best2 = _hill_climb_param(16384, p_best, p_low, p_high, 5000, hill2_end, rng)
                seq = _make_param_shape(16384, p_best2)
                sc, _ = _fft_score(seq)
                best_seq, best_score = _add_candidate(cands, best_seq, best_score, seq, sc)
                try:
                    print(f"hill 16384 score={sc:.8f} params={p_best2}", flush=True)
                except Exception:
                    pass
            except Exception:
                pass

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 4. Full-sequence local improvement on the best few templates.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    _sort_by_score(cands)
    local_start = time.monotonic()
    local_window = max(1.0, deadline - local_start - 25.0)

    for idx in range(min(2, len(cands))):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 20.0:
            break
        seq, sc = cands[idx][1], cands[idx][0]
        if idx == 0:
            sub_dl = local_start + max(10.0, 0.55 * local_window)
        else:
            sub_dl = deadline - 25.0
        sub_dl = min(sub_dl, deadline - 25.0)

        try:
            better_seq, better_sc = _improve_local(seq, sub_dl, rng)
            best_seq, best_score = _add_candidate(cands, best_seq, best_score, better_seq, better_sc)
            try:
                print(f"local candidate {idx} score={better_sc:.8f} len={int(better_seq.size)}", flush=True)
            except Exception:
                pass
        except Exception:
            pass

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 5. Final validity / exact spot check.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    _sort_by_score(cands)
    final_best_seq = best_seq.copy()
    final_best_score = best_score

    try:
        evaluator = evaluate_sequence  # noqa: F821
    except NameError:
        evaluator = None

    if evaluator is not None:
        _sort_by_score(cands)
        checked = 0
        for sc, seq in cands:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 4.0:
                break
            if checked >= 3:
                break
            if int(seq.size) > 12000:
                continue
            try:
                exact = float(evaluator([float(x) for x in np.asarray(seq, dtype=float)]))
                checked += 1
                if np.isfinite(exact) and (not np.isfinite(final_best_score) or exact < final_best_score - 1e-12):
                    final_best_score = exact
                    final_best_seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=float).copy()
            except Exception:
                pass

    # Ensure a finite, non-negative, positive-sum list.
    out = np.asarray(final_best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if out.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(out)):
        out = _make_fallback_sequence(4096)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 0.01:
        out = _normalize(_make_fallback_sequence(4096))
    else:
        # Keep the sum comfortably above zero and values bounded.
        out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
        out = out / out.sum()

    try:
        final_check = float(_score(out))
        print(f"final returned score (fast estimate)={final_check:.8f} len={int(out.size)}", flush=True)
    except Exception:
        pass

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
